## #3666: feat: Introduce plan/trainer config objects with merge helpers. (#2904)
**PR:** #3666 | **Last activity:** 2026-04-15T14:33:33Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @Sprengmeister-dev | **Comments:** 2 | **Created:** 2026-01-01 | **Labels:** autotune, hub, cuda tests, custom_dataloader, optional tests, internet tests, on-merge: backport to 1.4.x, nonJAX, mlflow | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3666

## Summary
  This PR introduces explicit training configuration objects for scvi-tools, reducing reliance on loose kwargs and improving clarity across training APIs.

  ## Changes
  - Added `TrainingPlanConfig`, `TrainerConfig`, and related config dataclasses with a shared merge helper.
  - Wired `plan_config` / `trainer_config` through training mixins, TrainRunner, and model training wrappers.
  - Updated docs to document the new configuration pattern and added a user guide page.
  - Added unit tests for config merging behavior.

  ## Testing
  - `python -m pytest tests/train/test_trainingplans.py`
  - `python -m pytest tests/train/test_config.py`

  ## Notes
  - `plan_kwargs` and `**trainer_kwargs` remain supported; explicit configs are additive.

**@ori-kron-wis:** close https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/issues/2904

**@ori-kron-wis:** Looks fine to me, thanks @Sprengmeister-dev

## #3665: feat: Introduce plan/trainer config objects with merge helpers. (#2904)
**PR:** #3665 | **Last activity:** 2026-01-01T19:32:06Z
**State:** closed | **Author:** @Sprengmeister-dev | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2026-01-01 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3665

## Summary
  This PR introduces explicit training configuration objects for scvi-tools, reducing reliance on loose kwargs and improving clarity across training APIs.

  ## Changes
  - Added `TrainingPlanConfig`, `TrainerConfig`, and related config dataclasses with a shared merge helper.
  - Wired `plan_config` / `trainer_config` through training mixins, TrainRunner, and model training wrappers.
  - Updated docs to document the new configuration pattern and added a user guide page.
  - Added unit tests for config merging behavior.

  ## Testing
  - `python -m pytest tests/train/test_trainingplans.py`
  - `python -m pytest tests/train/test_config.py`

  ## Notes
  - `plan_kwargs` and `**trainer_kwargs` remain supported; explicit configs are additive.

**State:** closed | **Author:** @Sprengmeister-dev | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2026-01-01 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3663

## Summary
  This PR introduces explicit training configuration objects for scvi-tools, reducing reliance on loose kwargs and improving clarity across training APIs.

  ## Changes
  - Added `TrainingPlanConfig`, `TrainerConfig`, and related config dataclasses with a shared merge helper.
  - Wired `plan_config` / `trainer_config` through training mixins, TrainRunner, and model training wrappers.
  - Updated docs to document the new configuration pattern and added a user guide page.
  - Updated tutorial notebooks to show `plan_config` usage.
  - Added unit tests for config merging behavior.

  ## Testing
  - `python -m pytest tests/train/test_trainingplans.py`
  - `python -m pytest tests/train/test_config.py`

  ## Notes
  - `plan_kwargs` and `**trainer_kwargs` remain supported; explicit configs are additive.
  - Notebooks updated in the `docs/tutorials/notebooks` submodule.

## #3482: chore(deps): add constraint to jax and jaxlib
**PR:** #3482 | **Last activity:** 2025-12-10T12:30:17Z
**State:** closed | **Author:** @TTTPOB | **Comments:** 1 | **Created:** 2025-08-22 | **Labels:** backlog | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3482

jax has announced deprecation of `from jaxlib.xla_extention import Device`. I've tested it still works in 0.6.0 but not in 0.6.2. `conda-forge` did not have a 0.6.1 package so I didn't test that.

Related url: https://github.com/jax-ml/jax/releases/tag/jax-v0.6.0

It said removal will happen in 0.7.0, but in my test, that was not the case.
<img width="1360" height="776" alt="image" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/0720cb80-59b6-4cad-820f-b92aadd4cd15" />

It the future, maybe we can consider move to public stable api.

**@ori-kron-wis:** Closing as we put JAX optional dep now

**@ori-kron-wis:** @Hrovatin made small fixes (pre-commit issue) + I had the transfrom_batch as a parameter in archesmixin, as we generally don't use globals in a model (except for classes names) and more models might benefit from this perhaps.
But perhaps youll want to emphasis this in the tutorial now.

beside that looks good. also tutorial. is there anything else missing? or we good to merge?

**@Hrovatin:** @ori-kron-wis  The purpose of the class attribute for preventing batch surgery in SysVI is that we want to disable surgery on batch whenever SysVI is used, as discussed [here](https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3448#discussion_r2264607523) with @canergen.
Changing it to a method parameter defeats its purpose. I would strongly push for having it as a class param as this is the only way I could find to message the user that model can not be used with batch surgery (raise error automatically). But if you have another suggestion on how to achieve this feel free to adjust.

EDIT: I guess another option would be to add a validator hook to that scarches function that I can override in SysVI if that fits better

**@ori-kron-wis:** > @ori-kron-wis The purpose of the class attribute for preventing batch surgery in SysVI is that we want to disable surgery on batch whenever SysVI is used, as discussed [here](https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3448#discussion_r2264607523) with @canergen. Changing it to a method parameter defeats its purpose. I would strongly push for having it as a class param as this is the only way I could find to message the user that model can not be used with batch surgery (raise error automatically). But if you have another suggestion on how to achieve this feel free to adjust.
>
> EDIT: I guess another option would be to add a validator hook to that scarches function that I can override in SysVI if that fits better

The reason is clear to me. Lets think of a solution that involves the …

**@Hrovatin:** @ori-kron-wis, do you have a suggestion on how to achieve it? All my ideas (above) require modification of scarches mixin

**@ori-kron-wis:** yes I just added load_query_data wrapper to sysvi with forcing transfer_batch=False. thats it.

**@Hrovatin:** @ori-kron-wis I fixed it a bit to ensure it is used properly and extended a test to test for it.

**@ori-kron-wis:** please fix the pre-commit errors so we can merge this

**@Hrovatin:** @ori-kron-wis done

## #3456: feat: CytoVI
**PR:** #3456 | **Last activity:** 2025-11-16T09:00:35Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @ori-kron-wis | **Comments:** 4 | **Created:** 2025-08-04 | **Labels:** on-merge: backport to 1.3.x | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3456

**@ori-kron-wis:** > Remove read/write functions from here and plotting code. Happy to discuss PyNNDescent use but then it should first be in base model and then we can add missing pieces here. Not convinced yet though. The mask layer in transformations is a bad idea, why not use code similar to totalVI here or at least a sparse matrix with 0 and 1.

Plotting functions should be specific for CytoVI , and applied in the tutorial (to come) as an example of how to do analysis with CytoVI. Obviously, we dont care for plotting in scvi-tools, but then the other option will be to implement them inside the tutorial. But if they will be heavily use, we could think to keep them in the code base.

**@canergen:** Currently, we mainly have those as _utils.py in the reproducibility repos and expect people to use those. It adds a lot of code to maintain and good plotting functions require quite some additional things. We also created a separate utils repo in the past: destvi_utils which broke. Maintaining it as tightly as scVI-tools adds a lot of burden.

**@ori-kron-wis:** ok. @florianingelfinger we can refer the tutorial to use those functions from the cytovi reproducible repo (need to obviously make it public) and then remove them from scvi-tools code base here.

Of course, like in the case of destvi and totalvi, those reproducibility repos codes tend to break with time as no-one manages them and knows how to fix them, so we need to carefully think this.

**@florianingelfinger:** We can move the read/write and plotting discussion offline. I think they are crucial to facilitate low entry hurdles for cytometry users for which there are much less established options available compared to other modalities.

## #3448: feat: adding scarches/classifier support in sysvi
**PR:** #3448 | **Last activity:** 2025-11-16T08:30:27Z
**State:** closed | **Author:** @ori-kron-wis | **Comments:** 11 | **Created:** 2025-07-31 | **Labels:** on-merge: backport to 1.3.x | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3448

**@ori-kron-wis:** Currently not available.
@Hrovatin, there is a demand for it. Do you want to contribute?

**@Hrovatin:** Hi @ori-kron-wis
If people are using the model and there is general demand I would be up for adding scarches.

With classifier do you mean the classier loss from scanvi or classification for cell type transfer?

However, someone would need to help with testing it as I only have access to a Mac (so someone running it on Linux would be need to make sure GPU support works as expected).

I assume I could just follow [src/scvi/model/_scvi.py](https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/blob/9e88eb162940fa2b63eb1c7478598d5e22c1df82/src/scvi/model/_scvi.py#L23) for scarches right?

**@ori-kron-wis:** @Hrovatin
Yes I think you can follow the implementation of scarches as with other models and the idea I was thinking of is mimicking the use of the classifier as with scanvi, but have its loss incorporated into the overall sysvi model "regular" loss.
Then, we could have it as a SysANVI model? or as an extension to the current one?

@canergen What are your thoughts?

You will have our support with full test suite checks, no worries.

**@canergen:** ScArches is very straightforward and just requires importing the correct class. There was request for sysANVI. @Hrovatin I would likely not built it, if you built it, it likely requires computing classification loss of both input as well as cyclic input classification loss, it could otherwise create weird behavior.

**@Hrovatin:** Yes, I would not feel comfortable adding the classification loss without being able to run benchmarks.

But I see the value of adding a tutorial on how to do (KNN) classification on top of any scvi-tools model (e.g. similar as someone suggested [here](https://discourse.scverse.org/t/conduct-scanvi-after-running-sysvi-on-multi-platform-data/3745/2)) - is this something you may be able to add on your side?

**@canergen:** https://docs.scvi-tools.org/en/1.3.3/tutorials/notebooks/hub/query_hlca_knn.html and the suggestion was me 👍

**@Hrovatin:** Ok then I will work on adding scarches and simply link that tutorial (or relevant place in it) at the end of the SysVI tutorial to show how it can be done.

**@ori-kron-wis:** I already put the scarches, you will just need to validate from your end.

**@Hrovatin:** Thank you @ori-kron-wis , will start testing it and adding to the tutorial now over the weekend.

**@Hrovatin:** @ori-kron-wis  please have a look at https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3466

**@Hrovatin:** @ori-kron-wis @canergen I also updated the tutorial https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tutorials/pull/468

## #3159: fix: nan failure during training
**PR:** #3159 | **Last activity:** 2025-08-18T08:22:27Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @ori-kron-wis | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2025-01-21 | **Labels:** Still Needs Manual Backport | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3159

Using SaveCheckpoint callback with on_exception can save the best optimal model up to the point it crashed due to Nan's in loss or gradients.
See an example (using Michal's data):

```
import scvi
from scvi.train._callbacks import SaveCheckpoint

lvae = scvi.model.SCANVI.from_scvi_model(

## #2868: style: add initial tensor shape typing to vae
**PR:** #2868 | **Last activity:** 2025-08-18T08:20:05Z
**State:** closed | **Author:** @martinkim0 | **Comments:** 1 | **Created:** 2024-06-28 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2868

closes #2541.

draft implementation of adding jaxtyping tensor shape annotation to vae

**@martinkim0:** Pros:
- Same syntax across JAX, PyTorch, and NumPy (not sure about Xarray or Sparse yet)
- Track shape annotations for tensors, which can make it easier to catch bugs earlier

Cons:
- Can be tedious to add typing to every function - probably need to decide when it's recommended and when it's optional
- Can sometimes lead to more verbose code

Other considerations:
- Need to use the same axis names across all the models for consistency, so need a good system to sync these and manage axis name conflicts

## #3276: tutorial index page with filterable list
**PR:** #3276 | **Last activity:** 2025-08-13T07:30:22Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @lordy5 | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2025-03-26 | **Labels:** on-merge: backport to 1.3.x | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3276

# Custom sphinx directives for filterable lists
Created custom sphinx directives which can be used to create a filterable list of cards, and used these directives to create a filterable list of tutorial cards for the tutorials index page.

These directives could also be used to create similar filterable lists easily if needed in other parts of the documentation.

## customcard
The first custom directive is `customcard` which is used to create, in this case, a card displaying information about a single scvi-tools tutorial. It has `path` parameter for specifying the path in the documentation to the tutorial, and a `tags` parameter for the tags describing that tutorial, which will be used for filtering in the list. The description for the tutorial goes in the directive's content.

This directive automatically generates the title, description, and group name that will be displayed on the card, as long as the correct path has been provided. The `customcard` directive should be placed a model group's index file (such as index_scrna.md).

Example:
```
\```{customcard}
:path: notebooks/scrna/harmonization
:tags: Analysis, Integration, Transfer-learning, Dimensionality-reduction, Removal-of-variance

This is a test description. Put the description as the main content of the customcard directive.
\```
```
## customlist
The second custom directive is `customlist` which creates a filterable list from all of the customcard directives. During the documentation build pro …

## #3230: Tutorials page alternative
**PR:** #3230 | **Last activity:** 2025-08-12T19:00:13Z
**State:** closed | **Author:** @lordy5 | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2025-03-03 | **Labels:** tutorials, on-merge: backport to 1.3.x | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3230

In this PR I created an alternative option for the tutorials index page. I think it is better than the previous option and has the following differences:

1. Broke up longer tutorial groups, like scRNA-seq into smaller subgroups, each with its own dropdown.
2. Used MyST cards instead of a list-table for each tutorial link. I think this looks less cluttered.
3. Used markdown headers so that users can easily navigate to the model type they want using the right sidebar.
4. Reordered tutorials and subgroups in a more logical way so that the page reads more like a tutorial.
5. Added descriptions for each subgroup so users have a better idea of whether those tutorials are relevant to them.

I think something like this layout is about as simplified as I can get with built in MyST while still allowing the user to skip to particular sections using the right sidebar. From the different options I've already tried, I think if there is an additional layer of subgroupings/labels/dropdowns then the page becomes too cluttered and is less helpful.

Still open for suggested changes and improvements of course.

![Screenshot 2025-03-03 at 8 07 07 PM](https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/6240212c-4b10-42bd-93f1-7f0991d238b3)

However, it looks like both `PyroJitGuideWarmup` and `PyroModelGuideWarmup` are just passing in a single minibatch through the guide prior to the training loop, so I like the idea of having a method like `setup_pyro_model` that does this. I think this makes more sense in the training plan though, using one of the Lightning hooks such as `on_train_start`. And there's definitely something weird going on with tensors on different devices, and I think using one of the Lightning hooks would solve this since their backend will …

**@vitkl:** Just a brief reply. Happy to have a zoom call about pyro.

Pyro automatic variational distribution (Guide) doesn’t have any parameters until you do a first pass through the model and guide. When moving my code to multi-GPU training I found that this needs to be done in setup step of the Lightning workflow - otherwise parameters created on GPU don’t get moved between devices correctly - so it’s it would not in on_train_start. However, in the latest version the setup step also doesn’t work - as reported in the original issue. Moving the code to this function and calling it before using any Lightning workflow steps seems to solve the problem for cell2location and my other project.

Actually the reason for the errors might be resolved if you call both the model and guide with one batch (it’s p …

**@canergen:** Please split into two PRs. One for the warmup changes and one for the inference changes. This makes it easier to follow changes.

## #3066: feat(external): implement METHYLANVI for scBS-seq data
**PR:** #3066 | **Last activity:** 2025-06-01T13:00:15Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @ethanweinberger | **Comments:** 7 | **Created:** 2024-12-03 | **Labels:** on-merge: backport to 1.3.x | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3066

@canergen per our email exchange, this PR adds my MethylANVI model implementation within `scvi.external.methylvi`.

**@ethanweinberger:** Hi @canergen @ori-kron-wis

I'm removing the draft label here for now to get your feedback before proceeding further on this PR. In short, the goal of this PR is to add an implementation of the MethylANVI (MethylVI + scANVI) model from the MethylVI manuscript. Beyond just the code necessary for MethylANVI, I've also created some new mixin's to capture shared functions between models and avoid too much code duplication; if it's easier for you I'm happy to move these to separate PRs. I provide more details below:

* MethylANVI model and module classes can be found in `scvi/external/methylvi`. To avoid duplicating shared functions between MethylVI/MethylANVI (e.g. `get_normalized_methylation`) I added a `BSSeqMixin` class in `external/methylvi/_base_components.py`. The `BSSeqMixin` essen …

**@canergen:** I like the idea of a SemisupervisedMixin class especially as we'll add more semisupervised models soon'ish. Predict should best case be part of it. However, the input to the classifier is slightly different for both scANVI and methylANVI. I'm also thinking about whether we should also abstract the module code for semisupervised models.
@ori-kron-wis can you also review this code? And provide your ideas on it?

**@ori-kron-wis:** Im in favor of reducing code duplications and having BSSeq & SemisupervisedTraining mixins.

I would suggest, as you both already pointed out, that as we expect more models, that are currently under development with "current" scanvi code, to use the SemisupervisedTraining mixin, I would create a new PR just for the scanvi changes here and concentrate only on Methylanvi in this PR, so our future integration will be easier.
The scanvi PR can be checked out from this branch.

It will have some code duplications for now until all other models will move also to SemisupervisedTrainingmixin (and probably expand it beyond methylvi).

I also validated the scnavi changes here, and it looks the same as before.

**@ethanweinberger:** Hi @canergen @ori-kron-wis. Happy new year! I just finished modifying this PR to address your comments (including reverting the previous changes to scANVI). Tests are currently failing, but the failures appear unrelated to this PR (the tests are related to general data loading functions).

@canergen per your suggestion I added a `predict` function to the `SemisupervisedMixin` class. I tried to make the function flexible to handle different numbers of inputs without requiring too many changes in other classes (see my comments). Let me know what you think! I'd also be happy to add a corresponding semisupervised module mixin to this PR, or I can open another PR after to avoid putting too much in one PR.

**@ori-kron-wis:** @ethanweinberger thanks!
The fail tests are a result of scipy version update integration issue with anndata. I noticed them.
https://github.com/scverse/anndata/issues/1811. Tests will fail until they will release their fix.

**@ori-kron-wis:** @ethanweinberger, we are merging this branch. If you can add a tutorial on this/extension to the already existing tutorial, it will be awesome. Thanks!

**@ethanweinberger:** @ori-kron-wis @canergen thank you so much for pushing this through! I've added updating the tutorial to my to-do list.

## #3204: docs: SysVI docs
**PR:** #3204 | **Last activity:** 2025-05-26T11:30:16Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @ori-kron-wis | **Comments:** 4 | **Created:** 2025-02-24 | **Labels:** on-merge: backport to 1.3.x | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3204

**@Hrovatin:** @ori-kron-wis thank you very much.
I see many of the other models have small tutorials in the md file and a lot of additional theory behind it. What would be your general guide on how much info we should add here, especially since we have a detailed tutorial in notebook.

**@ori-kron-wis:** @Hrovatin , well, it's up to you.
While the tutorial aim is to show an end-to-end typical use case of your model, the model description aim is to have a more theoretical background on it (see some other example, usually we expect to see advantages & limitations of model , the generative and inference processes scheme and tasks it can perform).
I can see your tutorial has some theoretical parts, it's fine, they can have some overlap between them.

As I said, it's up to you, we do have other external models with no theoretical background in RTD, which is a bit pitty, as it reduces the chances of users to really adopting the model.

**@Hrovatin:** @ori-kron-wis I added the model description here https://github.com/Hrovatin/scvi-tools/blob/Ori-sysvi-docs-ready/docs/user_guide/models/sysvi.md

Should I open a new PR for it?

The description I decide on is more general (without strict mathematical formulation) to appeal to users without CS background.

Also, I was not able to build the docs due to error with:

```
Extension error (sphinx.ext.autosummary):
Handler <function process_generate_options at 0x105df6d40> for event 'builder-inited' threw an exception (exception: no module named scvi.autotune)
```
So please have a look that the two added images display properly.

**@ori-kron-wis:** @Hrovatin thanks! looks great. I will take care on putting this in our RTD.

## #3189: fix: MrVI MixtureSameFamily log probability fixed
**PR:** #3189 | **Last activity:** 2025-05-24T20:30:06Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @justjhong | **Comments:** 1 | **Created:** 2025-02-13 | **Labels:** optional tests | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3189

Fixes https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/issues/3188.

This PR fixes the issue in both the `get_aggregated_posterior` function as well as the Mixture of Gaussians prior.

Tested on tutorial: integration remains the same, differential abundance results now take a much larger range as expected (see issue). To mitigate spikiness in favor of the sample of origin's covariate, we added the default omit_original_sample=True to avoid incorporating the sample of origin's log prob into the calculation.

CC @rastogiruchir

**@PierreBoyeau:** Quick observations:

- These changes do not seem to affect the performed analyses conducted in the paper. Here are the log ratios (COVID vs  healthy) from the **complete** Haniffa dataset, showing a similar behavior as in the MrVI manuscript.
![image](https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/2d3d12be-438a-4862-84d9-29bbde360a44)
- That being said, these log ratios seem slightly different when computed on the drastically subsampled Haniffa dataset. If similar issues are reported on small datasets, we might want to explore options to smooth log ratios then.

## #3125: feat: Adding multiGPU option to SCANVI
**PR:** #3125 | **Last activity:** 2025-05-21T11:30:18Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @ori-kron-wis | **Comments:** 6 | **Created:** 2025-01-07 | **Labels:** Still Needs Manual Backport, multiGPU tests | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3125

**@ori-kron-wis:** Running multigpu scanvi on an interactive session is not simple, because we usually need to train scvi first and then init the scanvi from it.
Better to do it all in one script and not from jupyter, but if we have to do in jupyter or other interactive shells (which make sense in order to see figures), we can do it in 2 different sessions (while saving and loading the scvi model using cpu) or 1 session but then to train the scvi with cpu. but we cant have scvi and scanvi running on the same interactive session with multiGPU (this is a lightning issue)

**@ori-kron-wis:** another caveat is that it cant run with early stopping callbacks (and some models, like totalvi, run it by default), so we disable it for DDP.

**@ori-kron-wis:** In order to run a multi-GPU training in an interactive session, like JupyetNB, the strategy should be:"ddp_notebook_find_unused_parameters_true", in such case, however, we cant see the model.history

**@canergen:** Does scANVI now work with partially labeled data? Does the subsample callback work? I don't see any code changes for those so I assume yes?

**@canergen:** We should mention it in the tutorial where things are changed now that this uses multi-GPU. We should create a page in our docs discussing multi-GPU.

**@ori-kron-wis:** > Does scANVI now work with partially labeled data? Does the subsample callback work? I don't see any code changes for those so I assume yes?

Yes. as discussed, we are having trouble seeing multigpu ddp running on pytest on remote servers. It works by just running Python scripts end to end in the multiGPU server or in a notebook (I might return to dig into this in the future). So right now, all is manually checked, and yes, it can work with partially labeled data or not.

The resampling works. the change was not in its callback itself or its sub-functions but in the fact of how dataloaders were concatenated when using ddp, this is seen in the changes done for files semi and concat dataloaders in the case of DDP use.

## #2826: compute DE+ and DE- separately
**PR:** #2826 | **Last activity:** 2025-05-20T08:00:28Z
**State:** closed | **Author:** @LevyNat | **Comments:** 7 | **Created:** 2024-06-03 | **Labels:** P0 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2826

**@canergen:** Fixed the wrong behavior that we have seen in different models (high significance despite low mean LFC but high standard deviation). It still requires a release note and it would be good to check in our DE tutorial whether things are changing there. I’m not sure that the Bayes factor computation is correct @PierreBoyeau?

**@PierreBoyeau:** Hi, LGTM. I am unsure about the Bayes factor here, since we don't have two, but three competing models now(equally expressed, upreg, and downreg). I would be in favor of not computing BFs in this case.

**@canergen:** I guess it's fine to remove. I would assume we do two tests: upregulated vs rest and downregulated vs rest. We output only the winner of the test for upregulated/downregulated. I guess the corresponding Bayes factor for the returned FDR is then: `upregulated/(equally+downregulated)` or `downregulated/(equally+upregulated)`.

**@LevyNat:** @canergen @PierreBoyeau can we merge to main?

I put the new proba_de computation in mode='change_threeway' and kept 'vanilla' as default. Just need to think about how we explain the fix in the documentation

**@canergen:** Put in other fixes, that we discussed when we met. Especially the threshold was computed wrong. Subset_idx is something that I need for resolVI and it needs to be in this function. The argument is also different now with two and three.

**@ori-kron-wis:** we need to rerun all tutorials that use DE once merged.

**@ori-kron-wis:** this branch was already merged in this PR: https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3144

## #3100: feat: Support MPS during training and inference
**PR:** #3100 | **Last activity:** 2025-03-31T08:00:07Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @ori-kron-wis | **Comments:** 2 | **Created:** 2024-12-17 | **Labels:** cuda tests | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3100

add support for Mac GPU (m1,m2,m3) for scvi and revert it to be default option when running from Mac (if available)

Testing are done manually on my MAC (has m3) + verification that still works on CPU/CUDA here

References:
https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/issues/132605
https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/issues/77764
https://discourse.scverse.org/t/macbook-m1-m2-mps-acceleration-with-scvi/2075/7

**@ori-kron-wis:** A comparison for training CPU, MPS M3 AND NVidia RTX 6000 Ada 48GB GDDR6
<img width="1160" alt="image" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/8a433309-bdb4-4926-af9f-68a475e9b4a7" />

**@canergen:** The difference was much bigger in my hands when increasing batch size (larger batch size creates larger matrix multiplications where MPS is more efficient than CPU) and compilation. It was actually faster than an A100 on Google Colab then. We should optimize this a bit.

## #3091: Add Decipher post-training methods
**PR:** #3091 | **Last activity:** 2025-03-22T19:30:13Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @justjhong | **Comments:** 2 | **Created:** 2024-12-13 | **Labels:** Still Needs Manual Backport, on-merge: backport to 1.3.x | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3091

Adds the following downstream methods to the Decipher class.
- `rotate_decipher_components`
- `compute_imputed_gene_expression`
- `compute_decipher_time`
- `compute_gene_patterns`
- All trajectory related methods

This is the remaining functionality desired to transfer from the original implementation.
Note, this implementation takes a more scvi-tools-esque approach to each of the methods,
avoiding saving results to the anndata object directly.

cc @ANazaret

**@canergen:** LGTM. Please merge other PR into this one and this one into main.

**@justjhong:** @canergen addressed the comments!

## #2842: feat: support numpy 2.0
**PR:** #2842 | **Last activity:** 2025-03-22T19:00:12Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @martinkim0 | **Comments:** 8 | **Created:** 2024-06-17 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2842

closes #2390

**@martinkim0:** this is currently blocked by the following upstream dependencies not supporting numpy 2.0:
- loompy
- pymde

**@Zethson:** The last release of pymde was in 2022 and the last commit a year ago. I am not sure how sustainable it is to keep depending on it...

**@canergen:** Agreed seems like lowly maintained. I am then also against keeping it optional and we should just highlight it in some tutorial. With RAPIDS UMAP the need is also much smaller now. Loompy still doesn't support >2.0 based on a quick check though. So let's wait until they have figured things out and drop pyMDE then.

**@canergen:** @ori-kron-wis Can you switch loom to anndata.io (https://anndata.readthedocs.io/en/latest/generated/anndata.io.read_loom.html)? I assume we then support numpy>2.0. We should for now make pyMDE optional and set an optional requirement to numpy<2.0 for this dependency. We should drop it for the 1.3 release.

**@ori-kron-wis:** @canergen
the issue is with numpy>2.0 + loompy.connect, which is barely maintained.

At anndata at one hand does not have a numpy upper limit constraint, and at the other has its own read_loom function that uses the same loompy.connect under the hood. Tried to use that and got the same error as above, @ilan-gold can confirm he sees that error in his tests.

Therefore, unless loompy will solve it (see https://github.com/linnarsson-lab/loompy/issues/186), I dont think theres much we can do but to issue warnings for the next release + to have it optional in tests, like census. See my commits.

As I mentioned,  cellxgene census package, which now uses tiledbsoma is also pinned for numpy<2.0

GimVI which is the only model uses loom should state it needs numpy<2.0 in its tutorial. …

**@canergen:** I assume loompy is a tutorial dependency? Let's get rid of loompy. I set up dvc yesterday with gdrive backend. We could switch all our tutorials to use our own files and store them as h5ad preprocessed. It will make us less dependent on some internet resources.
We don't need pyMDE anymore (I would argue rapids UMAP is good) and it's not well maintained. It's also quite inconsistent as it's utils code.
For cellXgene-census, we should raise an issue there.

**@ori-kron-wis:** I updated this branch and all loompy related things are gone.
Right now adata files are stored in figshare. As a 2nd step we might check the gdrive dvc , which Im still figuring out.
Also, those loompy tests took time , so now they are all under the --internet flag.

**@ori-kron-wis:** Pymde was removed from tutorials as well (see https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tutorials/pull/378)

## #3036: fix(train): in case of last batch <=2, move to validation if possible
**PR:** #3036 | **Last activity:** 2025-03-12T07:11:45Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @ori-kron-wis | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2024-10-31 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3036

In case that train_size is None and the size of the last batch during training is <=2 , we adaptively move those samples from training to validation if possible. If train_size is set by user we do no fix this error and let the user change its train_size, selected indices or use drop last batch option.

## #3044: feat: Add variance to ZINB model
**PR:** #3044 | **Last activity:** 2025-02-18T10:00:12Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @rvinas | **Comments:** 3 | **Created:** 2024-11-15 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3044

Implemented variance of ZINB distribution

**@canergen:** Thanks. Very appreciated. Do you have any link or math to clarify the computation?

**@canergen:** @ori-kron-wis I checked it an it's correct. Can you add a release note and merge it after the minified PR.

**@rvinas:** > Thanks. Very appreciated. Do you have any link or math to clarify the computation?

@canergen @ori-kron-wis Apologies for the delay and thank you for accepting my pull request!

I used the following NB parameterization:

$$
f_{NB}(x; r, p) = \frac{(x+r-1)!}{x!(r-1)!} (1-p)^x p^r
$$

The ZINB PMF is:

$$
f_{ZINB}(x; r, p, \pi) = (x == 0)\big(\pi + (1 - \pi) f_{NB}(x; r, p)\big) + (x > 0) (1 - \pi) f_{NB}(x; r, p)
$$

---
To calculate the ZINB variance, I wrote $V[x]$ as follows

$$
V[x] = E[x²] - E[x]² = E[x(x-1)] + E[x] - E[x]²
$$

and then computed $E[x]$ (the mean) and $E[x(x-1)]$.

---

First, here is how to calculate the mean $E[x]$ of a ZINB:

$$
\begin{align*}
E[x] &= \sum_{x=0}^{\infty} x \cdot f_{ZINB}(x; r, p, \pi) \\
&= (1 - \pi) \sum_{x=1}^{\ …

## #2834: feat(external): implement METHLYVI for scBS-seq data
**PR:** #2834 | **Last activity:** 2025-01-04T10:30:22Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @ethanweinberger | **Comments:** 14 | **Created:** 2024-06-10 | **Labels:** cuda tests | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2834

This PR implements methylation variational inference (methylVI) for modeling methylation data from single-cell bisulfite sequencing (scBS-seq) experiments, described originally in [Weinberger and Lee (2024).](https://openreview.net/forum?id=Mg2DM0F3AY). In short, methylVI assumes that counts of methylated cytosines across pre-defined genomic region features follow a [Beta-Binomial distribution](https://docs.scvi-tools.org/en/latest/api/reference/scvi.distributions.BetaBinomial.html). As a placeholder citation I put in a reference to the workshop paper with the intention of swapping in a citation to the full paper once we upload it to bioRxiv.

All implementation code can be found in `scvi/external/methylvi`. Of note, as part of this PR I've added a new `DecoderMETHYLVI` class in `external/methylvi/_base_components.py` for obtaining decoded Beta-Binomial parameters for BS-seq data. This class may be useful for the development of future models, and might be worth moving out of external. Otherwise, the bulk of the implementation code can be found in `scvi/external/methylvi/_model.py` and `scvi/external/methylvi/_module.py`.

I structured my implementation of methylVI so that the model could be used with a single methylation modality an AnnData object (e.g. CpG gene body features) or multiple modalities in a MuData object (e.g. both CpG and non-CpG gene body features as separate modalities). To avoid repeating too much code, under the hood I tried to treat models trained with …

**@ethanweinberger:** > Hi Ethan, taking over from Martin here. Can you help me with the definition of modalities. See my comments above. The rest LGTM.

Hey Can. Thanks for the feedback!

To clarify, a modality here represents a combination of cytosine context (e.g. CpG versus non-CpG) and sequence context (e.g. gene bodies versus promoter regions versus enhancer regions etc.). This interpretation of modality is similar to that previously used in the methylation section of the [MOFA+ paper](https://genomebiology.biomedcentral.com/articles/10.1186/s13059-020-02015-1). I see the easy potential for confusion though, so I've renamed references to "modality"/"modalities" to "context"/"contexts" instead.

While the Beta-Binomial likelihood is used for all of these contexts, I think it makes more sense to orga …

**@ethanweinberger:** > My suggestion is that we will merge it with the biorxiv version and the tutorials?

Fine with me! It'll probably take a couple of months before we're ready to put the full preprint on biorxiv. That said, if you're planning on releasing scvi-tools 1.2 soon and want to have methylVI ready for that release, I'm happy to merge before the preprint is ready. Whatever works for you is fine with me :)

In the meantime, please let me know what you think of my responses to your latest round of comments.

**@canergen:** Can yiu rebase to main? The tests should work now.

**@ethanweinberger:** @canergen just responded to your latest round of comments + rebased as requested. Let me know if you have any more feedback!

**@ori-kron-wis:** We need to fix this pre-commit ruff errors and then we can merge to v1.3

**@ethanweinberger:** @canergen @ori-kron-wis Thanks for the quick responses :)! I made your suggested change @canergen and fixed the ruff errors @ori-kron-wis.

As for the tutorial: I'm ready to open a PR on the tutorials repository whenever this PR is merged in.

## #3103: Internship Assignment : Batch Effect correction in Deconvolution of Spatial transcriptomics profiles
**PR:** #3103 | **Last activity:** 2024-12-18T16:10:35Z
**State:** closed | **Author:** @Mathixx | **Comments:** 1 | **Created:** 2024-12-18 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/3103

As per the Assignment, I have modified the following functions in order to implement a batch effect correction in the spatial deconvolution Modules of the RNAStereoscope model :

RNADeconv.init
RNADeconv._get_generative_inputs
RNADeconv.generative
RNADeconv.loss
RNAStereoscope.init
RNAStereoscope.setup_anndata

I have tested the following implementation in the following notebook : https://colab.research.google.com/drive/1guMxkfaDBrAXQavRVManOv5WlTPoFBvL?usp=sharing  where I follow the work done in https://docs.scvi-tools.org/en/stable/tutorials/notebooks/spatial/stereoscope_heart_LV_tutorial.html in order to show the efficiency of the Batch effect correction !

**@canergen:** It's usually conflicts (mainly changelog). If you do it manually, it shows you merge conflicts when you want to merge the branch into 1.2.X. Reason is mainly, we fork two branches of main, edit CHANGELOG in both and then try to merge both.

-   [x] If the changes are patches for a version, I have added the `on-merge: backport to x.x.x` label.

## Changes

Adds `scvi.dataloaders._samplers.BatchDistributedSampler`, a wrapper class around `torch.utils.data.distributed.DistributedSampler` that allows us to access a minibatch with a single call to the dataset's getitem. This helps with efficient indexing into sparse arrays.

**@martinkim0:** Hi @bkmartinjr, this PR includes the changes for enabling multi-GPU training with AnnDatas. Still need to test a bit more on our end, but feel free to experiment with this, feedback would be appreciated!

**@bkmartinjr:** @martinkim0 - I will have a look.  I'm a newb so it will take me a bit to sort out how to use (I didn't see any sample code or tests that use it to train a model -- if I missed something, just LMK)

**@martinkim0:** @bkmartinjr Ah sorry, I forgot to include an example of how to use this. You can use
```
model.train(strategy="ddp_find_unused_parameters_true")
```
and it will use the distributedsampler automatically. I'll also write some test cases covering training.

**@martinkim0:** @bkmartinjr

Re: DDP strategies
`"ddp_fork_find_unused_parameters_true"` does not work since the CUDA backend does not support the fork start method in multiprocessing, see [this article](https://pytorch.org/docs/stable/notes/multiprocessing.html).

Re: Early stopping metrics
Ah, this is because Lightning by default does not run validation steps. You can pass in this:
```
model.train(early_stopping=True, check_val_every_n_epoch=1)
```
You can also see the other available arguments for early stopping[here](https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/blob/main/scvi/train/_trainer.py#L95-L101).

We should probably set this automatically if `early_stopping` is passed in. Thoughts @adamgayoso?

**@martinkim0:** Support for concat and semisupervised dataloaders will be tackled in a different PR. This one just focuses on `AnnDataLoader`.

## #2805: Fix for get_normalized_expression
**PR:** #2805 | **Last activity:** 2024-10-24T08:30:27Z
**State:** closed | **Author:** @lauradmartens | **Comments:** 1 | **Created:** 2024-05-17 | **Labels:** P0 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2805

There is currently a problem when using the scvi model with `gene_likelihood='poisson'` and trying to get the normalized expression:

```
vae = scvi.model.SCVI(
    adata,
    gene_likelihood='poisson'
)
vae.get_normalized_expression(
    adata=adata,
    library_size="latent",
)
```
It throws the error:
```
RNASeqMixin.get_normalized_expression(self, adata, indices, transform_batch, gene_list, library_size, n_samples, n_samples_overall, weights, batch_size, return_mean, return_numpy, **importance_weighting_kwargs)
    257 inference_kwargs = {"n_samples": n_samples}
    258 inference_outputs, generative_outputs = self.module.forward(
    259     tensors=tensors,
    260     inference_kwargs=inference_kwargs,
    261     generative_kwargs=generative_kwargs,
    262     compute_loss=False,
    263 )
--> 264 exp_ = getattr(generative_outputs["px"], generative_output_key)
    265 exp_ = exp_[..., gene_mask]
    266 exp_ *= scaling


AttributeError: 'Poisson' object has no attribute 'mu'
```

In the current implementation the `get_normalized_expression` fails for all likelihood that are not `zinb/nb` because `generative_outputs["px"]` does not have the attribute `mu`.

I tried to make a fix for the Poisson likelihood but I am unsure what exactly you would like to use for the normal distribution.

**@canergen:** Hi Laura, LGTM. Sorry for the late reply. I think it would make things clearer to define mu=rate within the reconstruction losses, so every reconstruction loss has a "mu". I went ahead and implemented this in #2915. The code will be released in scvi-tools version 1.2.

## #2913: fix(module) TotalVI fix activation background
**PR:** #2913 | **Last activity:** 2024-10-24T04:00:26Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @canergen | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2024-07-26 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2913

In the totalVI decoder, we were stacking two exponential activation functions. This was producing numerical instabilities. We replace this with a softplus activation function to increase numerical instability. Code is backwards compatible. Default has changed.

## #1957: Modifications to scVI that improve preservation of biological variance while correcting known tech effects
**PR:** #1957 | **Last activity:** 2024-09-09T17:49:42Z
**State:** closed | **Author:** @vitkl | **Comments:** 3 | **Created:** 2023-03-14 | **Labels:** P2 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/1957

Implementing changes proposed in https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/issues/1903#issuecomment-1466746644

1. [library variance weight](https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/1957/commits/ae9899aa12ae0d5477986f17babc697f282a6193) the prior `l_sigma` is an overestimate of the total variance attributed to the technical effect. A potential fix (and an easy fix) would be adding a hyperparameter to allow the user to reduce variance using a simple weight.

2. [softplus library activation](https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/1957/commits/17c04c3b140e973704d757ee2668a4819378f30b) Indeed it is possible that softplus would make the computation more stable.

3. [library_n_hidden](https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/1957/commits/6700b86a8b881e5e7c497d3400e71f7e46dd1c02) I assume that the encoder network size, n_hidden is the same both for `z` and `l` which in my example is a pretty large number. This would mean that the network is massively overparameterised. In my trials of amortizing inference for cell2location I observed that such 1d parameters need to be amortized with a much smaller network to achieve numerical stability and avoid loss increase (n_hidden=10). Is it possible to change n_hidden exclusively for this parameter? If yes I would like to try this and report results.

4. [not use_observed_lib_size means scale_activation="softplus"](https://github.com/vitkl/scvi-tools/commit/ef3978ade7e02a5ea48c4ae8810d90c180fc9fd1)

**@vitkl:** Hi @adamgayoso

Would be great to know what you think about these changes

**@vitkl:** Summary of the changes

1. Additive background term for every batch (batch_key):
        * Correcting for ambient RNA while not requiring the use of empty droplets & associated scalability/access issues
        * Combines cell2location batch correction features with scVI, but requires changing decoder transformation to softplus, encoder for y_c:
                   `d_cg ~ NB((mu_cg + s_eg) * y_c, alpha_eg)`              `d_cg ~ NB(mu_cg * y_c, alpha_eg)`
                   `mu_cg = softplus(DecoderNN(latent_cf))`                  `mu_cg = softmax_c(DecoderNN(latent_cf))`
                   `y_c = EncoderNN(d_cg, n_hidden=32)`
2. Regularising overdispersion alpha_eg to push NB towards Poisson:
        * Adding regularization term to loss
2. More constrained decoder not using batch …

**@canergen:** Closing here per our discussion. Please reopen when you are ready to merge it.

## #2578: Reference mapping with MuData-based models
**PR:** #2578 | **Last activity:** 2024-09-01T23:30:41Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @ethanweinberger | **Comments:** 3 | **Created:** 2024-02-29 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2578

(Addresses #2393) While certain multimodal models (e.g. totalVI) can be used with either `AnnData` or `MuData` objects, currently the `ArchesMixin` class only supports `AnnData`-based models and so reference mapping cannot be performed with `MuData`-based models. This PR thus extends `ArchesMixin` class to handle MuData-based models by making appropriate changes to the `load_query_data` function and adding a new `prepare_query_mudata` function.

@martinkim0 my initial PR is still a work-in-progress (e.g. no unit tests yet), but I thought it would be helpful to get some initial feedback from you. To avoid duplicating code, my instinct for the `prepare_query_mudata` function was to essentially be a lightweight wrapper that calls `prepare_query_anndata` for each modality in the `MuData` object. However, when attempting to do this naively I quickly ran into issues with `var_names` for `MuData` objects being saved as a single list for all modalities. In particular, because variable names don't have identifiers for their modality of origin, I ran into issues where e.g. a protein-modality-`AnnData` would erroneously be padded with zeros for "missing" RNA-modality features. To work around this, I modified the base `save` function such that variable names are saved as a dictionary (with modalities as keys) for `MuData` objects.

This worked (as in, my code ran without errors and current tests still pass), but I imagine this would lead to backwards incompatibility issues for previo …

**@ethanweinberger:** @martinkim0 with the closing of #2769, I've gone ahead and taken another stab at this PR.

The biggest change now compared to main is that I factored out most of the old code in `prepare_query_anndata` into a new function `_pad_and_sort_query_anndata` that's reused across `prepare_query_anndata` and a new `prepare_query_mudata` method.

Let me know what you think/if there's anything else you'd like in this PR in terms of testing/documentation/etc.

**@ethanweinberger:** Hi @martinkim0. Just wanted to check in to see if you had any feedback for the latest version of this PR. Thanks!

**@ethanweinberger:** > Thanks a lot! This looks great - I think refactoring out `_pad_and_sort_query_anndata` makes a lot of sense. Just left some minor comments regarding typing and getting var names.
>
> One last thing: could you add a test for `prepare_query_mudata` as well as a release note in `CHANGELOG.md` (this can go under the v1.2 release).

Added the requested typing + incorporated your suggestion on `._get_var_names`. For testing I added a new test `test_scarches_mudata_prep_layer` in `test_totalvi.py` analogous to `test_scarches_data_prep_layer` in `test_scvi.py`.

## #1074: modify DE results dataframe to have columns "group1" and "group2" in addition to "comparison"
**PR:** #1074 | **Last activity:** 2024-08-22T15:34:54Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @Munfred | **Comments:** 4 | **Created:** 2021-05-26 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/1074

I often work with big dataframes of concatenated DE results among arbitrary groups, and so I like to have the names of the group1 and group2 labels in separate columns, instead of joined by a " vs " string, eg `Endothelial vs Fibroblast`.

So I often end up doing:
```
de_df['group1']=de_df['comparison'].str.split('vs', expand=True)[0]
de_df['group2']=de_df['comparison'].str.split('vs', expand=True)[1]
```
 I made a small change so that the de_df result is now in this format already, for example the output from the api_overview tutorial would be:

```
de_df = model.differential_expression(
    groupby="cell_type",
    group1="Endothelial",
    group2="Fibroblast"
)
de_df.head()

Out[16]:
        proba_de  proba_not_de  ...       group1      group2
ABTB2     0.9646        0.0354  ...  Endothelial  Fibroblast
DANT2     0.9642        0.0358  ...  Endothelial  Fibroblast
TMBIM4    0.9640        0.0360  ...  Endothelial  Fibroblast
LRRTM3    0.9640        0.0360  ...  Endothelial  Fibroblast
CLDN5     0.9638        0.0362  ...  Endothelial  Fibroblast
```

I implemented this as a breaking change, however if it is preferable I can instead make it an additional argument with the default as the old behavior.

**@adamgayoso:** We were actually meaning to do this. Can we maybe for now keep both? Then if we keep both we can add a deprecation message or just keep both because it's just one column

**@Munfred:** Keeping both seems like a good idea for now, but shouldn't it be two new columns? `res["group1"] = g1` and `res["group2"] = g2`

**@adamgayoso:** > Keeping both seems like a good idea for now, but shouldn't it be two new columns? res["group1"] = g1 and res["group2"] = g2

Yes! my change didn't affect your addition of g2, you don't have to accept my change if you just want to do it yourself.

**@Munfred:** Ah! My bad thanks!

## #2718: Backport PR #2692 on branch 1.2.x (Add Beta-Binomial distribution for modeling scBS-seq data)
**PR:** #2718 | **Last activity:** 2024-06-21T16:09:39Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @meeseeksmachine | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2024-04-11 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2718

Backport PR #2692: Add Beta-Binomial distribution for modeling scBS-seq data

## #2692: Add Beta-Binomial distribution for modeling scBS-seq data
**PR:** #2692 | **Last activity:** 2024-06-21T16:09:39Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @ethanweinberger | **Comments:** 6 | **Created:** 2024-04-05 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2692

New assays (e.g. snmC-seq) have emerged for measuring DNA methylation using bisulfite sequencing (BS-seq). Due to issues with coverage (i.e., different cytosines are measured for each cell), BS-seq measurements at individual cytosines are often aggregated across larger region features (e.g. gene bodies, enhancers, etc.) before performing computational analyses. Due to observed overdispersion, when modeling these sums of binary cytosine-level measurements, the Beta-Binomial distribution is commonly used ([1](https://genomebiology.biomedcentral.com/articles/10.1186/s13059-021-02329-8), [2](https://openreview.net/forum?id=Mg2DM0F3AY)).

To facilitate prototyping future models of BS-seq data, this PR adds the Beta-Binomial with mean-dispersion parameterization to the scvi-tools collection of distributions.

@martinkim0 @canergen As discussed previously I added the Beta-Binomial distribution to the main `scvi` folder (as opposed to external). Let me know what you'd like to see in terms of testing: because the class is just a thin wrapper around Pyro's Beta-Binomial distribution, I wasn't sure what would be appropriate here.

**@canergen:** LGTM. Can you add tests for the function using both parametrizations? I left two comments.

**@ethanweinberger:** > LGTM. Can you add tests for the function using both parametrizations? I left two comments.

Happy to add tests, but what tests are you thinking specifically? Something like: likelihood values are the same for an $(\alpha, \beta)$ pair and the corresponding $(\mu, \gamma)$ pair?

**@canergen:** I would check that log_probability of both parametrizations is close. It'd be also sufficient though to test the ValueError (set all parameters to None) and param1 (just check that you can sample from the distribution and it has a finite log_probability). I assume both parts of the code are not reached in methylVI.

**@ethanweinberger:** > I would check that log_probability of both parametrizations is close. It'd be also sufficient though to test the ValueError (set all parameters to None) and param1 (just check that you can sample from the distribution and it has a finite log_probability). I assume both parts of the code are not reached in methylVI.

Added tests checking:

1. log_prob of both parameterizations is close, and
2. ValueError is raised if a parameterization is not specified

**@martinkim0:** Forgot to mention in my review: it would also be nice to have a release note under `CHANGELOG.md`. Thanks!

**@ethanweinberger:** @martinkim0 just finished addressing your comments. Let me know if there's anything else you'd like from me on this PR

## #2626: feat(model): support batch key in condscvi
**PR:** #2626 | **Last activity:** 2024-06-21T16:09:38Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @martinkim0 | **Comments:** 4 | **Created:** 2024-03-22 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2626

closes #2623

**@martinkim0:** @canergen The main backwards compatibility concern right now is that, with the current `AnnDataManager` setup with a batch key, the model will receive a tensor of zeroes for `batch_index` even if `batch_key` is `None`.

There's not a straightforward way to distinguish this case from the case where a `batch_key` is registered but all observations have the same batch assignment. However, we'd like to treat the latter differently from the former because of backwards compatibility.

This is because a `batch_index` of zeros will still be passed into the encoder (if `encode_covariates` is `True`) and the decoder, and will affect model training. So anyone repeating model training from a previous version will see different results.

There are two potential ways to address this:
1. Modify …

**@martinkim0:** @canergen Here's an initial exploration with using the batch key. TLDR Seems fine but can lead to overintegration when training for too long.
[condscvi_batch.pdf](https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/files/14763827/condscvi_batch.pdf)

**@martinkim0:** Losses are very similar across the runs:
<img width="1079" alt="Screenshot 2024-03-26 at 12 35 32" src="https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/assets/46072231/01ca3abb-3d35-4078-9a41-f995a9a4e5a6">

**@canergen:** We have quite some different datasets to see better integration. You need to run one UMAP per coarse celltype above. I don't think those are necessary but I requested only support to get the key into DestVI :).

## #2561: refactor `scvi.autotune` with functional API
**PR:** #2561 | **Last activity:** 2024-06-21T16:09:35Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @martinkim0 | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2024-02-26 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2561

- Remove `Tunable` and `TunableMixin` in favor of no validation checks of search space
- Remove `ModelTuner` in favor of functional API with `run_autotune`
- Remove `TunerManager` in favor of `AutotuneExperiment` that implements similar checks with no validation of metrics or parameters

## #2484: Backport PR #2467 on branch 1.1.x (Add API for custom dataloaders in `SCVI`)
**PR:** #2484 | **Last activity:** 2024-06-21T16:09:34Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @meeseeksmachine | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2024-02-07 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2484

Backport PR #2467: Add API for custom dataloaders in `SCVI`

## #2467: Add API for custom dataloaders in `SCVI`
**PR:** #2467 | **Last activity:** 2024-06-21T16:09:33Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @martinkim0 | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2024-02-05 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2467

This commit enables users to bypass the default workflow of setting up an `AnnData` and initializing a model with it in order to train, and instead being able to train a model directly with a custom dataloader.

- Make `adata` argument optional in `SCVI`, which sets a `_module_init_on_train` flag (through `BaseModelClass`) if no `adata` is passed in
- This causes the module init to be delayed until train time since we won't have access to certain arguments (*e.g.* `n_input` and `n_batch`) that must be inferred from the data
- Add an optional `data_module` argument to `UnsupervisedTrainingMixin.train` that must be a `LightningDataModule` and must be passed in if the model was not initialized with `adata`

Set as an experimental feature for now. Will expand to models other than `SCVI` when the implementation and API stabilize.

**PR:** #2738 | **Last activity:** 2024-05-01T20:47:21Z

## #2696: Save per-modality feature names for MuData models
**PR:** #2696 | **Last activity:** 2024-05-01T20:45:48Z
**State:** closed | **Author:** @ethanweinberger | **Comments:** 3 | **Created:** 2024-04-08 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2696

Current MuData-based models save their `var_names` as a single list, with no information on which feature came from which modality. This is problematic for certain downstream features (e.g. reference mapping [#2393](https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/issues/2393)) which require knowledge of features' modalities. This PR thus modifies the base `save` function to save `var_names` as a dictionary for MuData models, where each key is a modality and each value is that modality's features. In anticipation of potential backwards compatibility issues, this PR also includes a `convert_legacy_mudata_save` method to update earlier MuData models saved under the previous scheme.

@martinkim0 @canergen Per our discussion I added convenience functions for converting legacy models saved with the old MuData var names saving scheme (as opposed to just waiting until scvi-tools 2.0 and not worrying about backwards compatibility). I made this a separate PR from the scArches MuData PR (https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2578) to keep each PR more focused/reduce reviewer burden.

**@martinkim0:** Just to add to my comment above, we do some validation checking that the saved var names match with the loaded mudata in `load`.

In the case where an old model save is loaded with new code, we can just check that the union of per-mod var names in the mudata is equal to the var names in the model save (since I believe the top level var names in mudata corresponds to the union of all mods).

**@ethanweinberger:** Hi @martinkim0

Your suggested path forward largely makes sense to me. My one question is: what's the logic behind raising a user warning with the new default save scheme (i.e., saving per-mod var names for new mudata models)?

**@martinkim0:** sorry for the delay - I've merged support for this with #2769. thanks for putting in this initial pr! i'll close this now.

## #2769: feat(model): save mudata var names per modality
**PR:** #2769 | **Last activity:** 2024-05-01T20:43:24Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @martinkim0 | **Comments:** 1 | **Created:** 2024-05-01 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2769

previously, mudata-based models (totalvi and tangram) saved variable names in a flat array with values concatenated across all modalities - this is what happens when MuData.var_names is called directly. this is an issue for performing scarches on such a model as we cannot pad with zeros without being able to map individual variable names back to their respective modalities.

this commit sets the default to saving per-modality var names as a dictionary, with keys corresponding to modality keys and values to variable names. additionally, it adds a flag to the save method in case a user wants to use the legacy saving format.

old model saves will still be loaded successfully as the code will detect if the legacy save was used and validate var_names accordingly.

BREAKING CHANGE: changes the default saving scheme for mudata-based models. see above for details.

**@martinkim0:** edge case: if a user decides to change a modality between saving and loading a model (_including_ modalities not used directly), this will raise an error on our end. this issue is also present in the old code.

the solution would be to only save var names for modalities registered with the model - will be tackled in a separate pr.

**PR:** #2187 | **Last activity:** 2024-04-19T15:19:40Z
**State:** closed | **Author:** @martinkim0 | **Comments:** 4 | **Created:** 2023-07-17 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2187

-   [ ] Closes #xxxx (Replace xxxx with the GitHub issue number)
-   [ ] Tests added and passed if fixing a bug or adding a new feature
-   [ ] All code checks passed.
-   [ ] Added type annotations to new arguments/methods/functions.
-   [ ] Added an entry in the latest `docs/release_notes/index.md` file if fixing a bug or adding a new feature.
-   [ ] If the changes are patches for a version, I have added the `on-merge: backport to x.x.x` label.

**@martinkim0:** Fails because the latest numba (0.57.1) and chex (0.1.81) have incompatible numpy dependencies (<=1.24 and >=1.25 respectively).

See the numba tracker for 1.25 support, which should fix this: https://github.com/numba/numba/issues/8698. Numba 0.58 expected to release on the week of 2023-08-07

**@jykr:** chex<=0.1.8 is not compatible with the newest version of Jax (0.4.26) due to the use of KeyArray (see https://github.com/google-deepmind/chex/issues/332, https://github.com/google-deepmind/chex/compare/v0.1.8...master), leading to installation error. Updating to chex 0.1.86 resolved the issue.

**@canergen:** In the newest release there is no dependency on chex anymore in scVI-tools but we instead removed the uses of it. Are you facing issues with the newest release? Can you check your version?

**@jykr:** Ah I was working on the previous version (1.1.0), thanks!

## #2689: fix(external): ensure solo soft predict returns probs
**PR:** #2689 | **Last activity:** 2024-04-08T17:17:38Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @martinkim0 | **Comments:** 4 | **Created:** 2024-04-04 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2689

BREAKING CHANGE: SOLO.predict used to incorrectly return logits instead of probabilities when soft=True (the default option). this pr fixes that but it is a breaking change.

closes #2688

**@canergen:** It needs to be labeled as breaking change. It might be some folks use softmax after SOLO (I do). I guess it's better to also print a warning about the change for scVI-tools 1.3.

**@martinkim0:** > It needs to be labeled as breaking change. It might be some folks use softmax after SOLO (I do). I guess it's better to also print a warning about the change for scVI-tools 1.3.

Right - I forgot to do that. This is slated to be released with 1.1.3, should we revert it then? It's technically a bug fix so IMO it should be fine.

**@canergen:** I think it's fine to release with 1.1.3 and I prefer outputting probabilities. I agree the code was wrong and not the documentation. However, we should highlight this change as it might affect analysis scripts. I'm open whether it outputs a warning or not but the release note should contain a breaking change.

**@martinkim0:** Makes sense! I'll add a warning and a note to the changelog.

## #2644: chore(module): remove unused code paths in scanvae loss
**PR:** #2644 | **Last activity:** 2024-04-03T16:06:20Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @martinkim0 | **Comments:** 5 | **Created:** 2024-04-01 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2644

BREAKING CHANGE: removes unused code paths in the loss method of SCANVAE due to the default being `feed_labels=False`. internal functions that call this method either have their own default set to `feed_labels=False` or have `feed_labels=True` but don't pass it into the loss method (e.g. compute_elbo).

also adds typing within the modified file.

closes #2134

**@martinkim0:** per our discussion in slack @canergen

**@martinkim0:** sanity check with the [Atlas-level integration of lung data](https://docs.scvi-tools.org/en/stable/tutorials/notebooks/scrna/harmonization.html) tutorial:

[harmonization.pdf](https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/files/14829624/harmonization.pdf)

**@canergen:** LGTM! Would an old model still load? I guess so as all of these feed_labels are created dynamically. Otherwise, we need to update the scvi-hub scANVI models.

**@canergen:** Not sure it's breaking in this case as it was never active.

**@martinkim0:** Yeah I think it should be backwards compatible - we have tests for old scanvi loads.

## #2669: chore(misc): update direct dependencies
**PR:** #2669 | **Last activity:** 2024-04-03T00:07:56Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @martinkim0 | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2024-04-02 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2669

adds the following as direct dependencies:

- tensorboard>=2.0 for upcoming #2645
- sparse>=0.14.0 for upcoming #2549 (it's also currently optional for cricitism)
- xarray>=2023.2.0 for upcoming #2549 (it's also currently optional for cricitism)

removes the following direct dependency specifications:

- optax: required by flax
- numpy>=1.21.0: anndata requires numpy>=1.23
- pandas>=1.0: anndata requires pandas>=1.4, !=2.1.0rc0, !=2.1.2
- scipy: anndata requires scipy>1.8, <1.13.0rc1
- h5py>=2.9.0: anndata requires h5py>=3.1
- tqdm>=4.56.0: lightning requires tqdm>=4.57.0, <4.67.0
- jax>=0.4.4: required by flax
- jaxlib>=0.4.3: required by flax
- torch>=1.8.0: lightning requires torch>=1.13.0, <2.3.0
- anndata>=0.7.5: mudata requires anndata>=0.8

these dependencies are only removed because other direct dependencies (1) also have them as direct dependencies and (2) have tighter version bounds/restrictions than we currently have.

## #2517: Add changes for tracking scib-metrics in autotune
**PR:** #2517 | **Last activity:** 2024-03-02T00:55:06Z
**State:** closed | **Author:** @martinkim0 | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2024-02-16 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2517

Here's an example code snippet for how this works with SCVI:
```
import scvi
from scvi.autotune import ModelTuner
from scvi.train._scib_callback import ScibCallback

adata = scvi.data.synthetic_iid()
model_cls = scvi.model.SCVI
model_cls.setup_anndata(adata, batch_key="batch", labels_key="labels")

tuner = ModelTuner(model_cls)
tuner.fit(
    adata,
    use_defaults=True,
    metric="validation Batch correction",  # necessary
    train_kwargs={"callbacks": [ScibCallback(stage="validation")]},  # necessary
    validate_metrics=False,  # necessary
)
```
Code is not nice yet as the `TrainingPlan` has to accumulate and store the raw counts, latent representation, batches, and labels for this to work.

## #1951: Fix fdr gene tagging when integers in var names/obsm columns
**PR:** #1951 | **Last activity:** 2024-03-01T17:45:57Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @adamgayoso | **Comments:** 1 | **Created:** 2023-03-10 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/1951

For more context see the discussion on discourse:

https://discourse.scverse.org/t/questions-about-running-differential-expression/1137/8


When using totalVI, if protein counts are in obsm as np array and no column names are provided, we generate sequential integers. This causes indexing issues in the fdr tagging as the index is mixed between gene names and the integers.

**@adamgayoso:** I just want @PierreBoyeau to give it a quick look

## #2558: Backport PR #2557 on branch 1.2.x (remove `reporter` argument in `ModelTuner`)
**PR:** #2558 | **Last activity:** 2024-02-26T18:27:07Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @meeseeksmachine | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2024-02-26 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2558

Backport PR #2557: remove `reporter` argument in `ModelTuner`

## #2557: remove `reporter` argument in `ModelTuner`
**PR:** #2557 | **Last activity:** 2024-02-26T18:26:28Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @martinkim0 | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2024-02-26 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2557

As of Ray 2.6, its backend will auto-detect the context it was called in (whether that be an interactive session or otherwise) and adjust the progress reporter accordingly. This was previously done our end, so the `reporter` argument is no longer needed. See https://github.com/ray-project/ray/issues/36949

## #2472: Make xarray and sparse optional deps, remove chex dep, remove Pandas pin, support newest Ray, temp remove cellxgene dep
**PR:** #2472 | **Last activity:** 2024-02-12T20:45:58Z
**State:** closed | **Author:** @martinkim0 | **Comments:** 1 | **Created:** 2024-02-07 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2472

- Change `xarray` and `sparse` to optional dependencies as they're only used for `RNAMixin.posterior_predictive_sample` and `PPC`
- Remove `chex` dependency (planning on adding this back as a test dependency once we have a better JAX testing framework) by changing `TuneAnalysis` to the standard dataclass and `LossOutput` as a `flax.struct.dataclass`
- Remove pin on Pandas as #2308 is resolved now
- Temporarily remove cellxgene-census dep
- Update autotune for new ray API

Need to find a cleaner way to modify `LossOutput` attributes post init.

**@martinkim0:** It's possible that cellxgene-census's upperbound of numpy < 1.25 is causing a lot of dependency issues as removing it leads to dependencies installing smoothly https://github.com/chanzuckerberg/cellxgene-census/issues/979

## #2447: Backport PR #2446 on branch 1.1.x (Fix reinitialization error in GIMVI)
**PR:** #2447 | **Last activity:** 2024-01-29T17:39:05Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @meeseeksmachine | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2024-01-29 | **Labels:** cuda tests | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2447

Backport PR #2446: Fix reinitialization error in GIMVI

## #2446: Fix reinitialization error in GIMVI
**PR:** #2446 | **Last activity:** 2024-01-29T17:38:38Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @martinkim0 | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2024-01-26 | **Labels:** cuda tests | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2446

Fixes #2273. Error occurs when re-initializing GIMVI with the same spatial dataset due to the following code in `GIMVI.__init__` that in-place modified an `.obs` column:
```
adata_seq_n_batches = sum_stats[0]["n_batch"]
adata_spatial.obs[
    self.adata_managers["spatial"]
    .data_registry[REGISTRY_KEYS.BATCH_KEY]
    .attr_key
] += adata_seq_n_batches
```
This was necessary as the spatial batches and GEX batches were 0-indexed, but we wanted to distinguish between them. The fix checks whether the obs column's minimum is 0, and if so, we add the offset. Otherwise, we don't, since that means the column was updated before within the same session.

## #2398: Backport PR #2395 on branch 1.1.x (Fix repr for custom distributions, add optional constraint)
**PR:** #2398 | **Last activity:** 2024-01-17T22:56:28Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @meeseeksmachine | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2024-01-17 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2398

Backport PR #2395: Fix repr for custom distributions, add optional constraint

## #2395: Fix repr for custom distributions, add optional constraint
**PR:** #2395 | **Last activity:** 2024-01-17T22:48:21Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @martinkim0 | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2024-01-17 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2395

Fixes #2392

`NegativeBinomial` and `ZeroInflatedNegativeBinomial` would raise an ambiguous `ValueError` when the following conditions were met:

- `scale` not passed in and thus defaulted to `None`
- `log_prob` called on a tensor not within support

This caused an error on [this line](https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/torch/distributions/distribution.py#L316) when calling [`__repr__`](https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/main/torch/distributions/distribution.py#L332) since `self.scale` evaluated to `None`.

Another issue was that `validate_args` could not be `True` when any optional parameters are left as `None`. Fixed with an optional constraint wrapper.

**@martinkim0:** I'm still confused as to whether criticism metrics should use `model.adata` or the user-inputted `adata`. **@canergen:** @watiss You were opposed about a default to model.adata. We should then check that model.adata is not minified, in which case the default doesn't make sense.

## #2297: Fix a bug in the imputation process of Gimvi
**PR:** #2297 | **Last activity:** 2023-12-09T00:26:18Z
**State:** closed | **Author:** @HelloWorldLTY | **Comments:** 6 | **Created:** 2023-10-23 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2297

Hi, this is my current solution for this bug:

https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/issues/1634

I think we need to add a key about batch_size in the model.py part  to ensure that the user can adjust the batch_size they used for inference. Moreover, we also need a warning if one modality has data size which will lead to one example remining problem.

**@HelloWorldLTY:** Any feedback?

**@martinkim0:** Hi, thank you for putting in this PR. Regarding adding `batch_size`, I believe we already expose that argument in both the `train` method as well as all inference method. Were you referring to a different method?

I agree that it's probably best to include some sort of warning if there ends up being a single observation in a batch since this leads to issues with batch norm. However, it's not clear to me why this is occurring during inference. Could you provide a reproducible example that leads to this error? Thank you.

**@kamilkrukowski:** There's code dependent on ```_make_scvi_dls``` that expects the ```batch_size``` parameter to do something instead of nothing.

```get_latent_representation``` and ```get_imputed_values```  iterate over the data loaders created by ```_make_scvi_dls``` and the batch_size parameter of ```get_imputed_values``` is only passed to ```_make_scvi_dls``` and otherwise unused.

```
342        scdls = self._make_scvi_dls(adatas, batch_size=batch_size)
343
344        imputed_values = []
345        for mode, scdl in enumerate(scdls):
346            imputed_value = []
```

Thus, currently the parameter ```batch_size``` is completely ignored for ```get_latent_representation```, which seems like unintentional buggy behavior resolved by the PR.

About the "batch_size of 1" issue --> If we hav …

**@martinkim0:** Thanks, I didn't catch that `batch_size` was not being passed into `_make_scvi_dls`. Regarding the second point, I'm aware of this but afaik it should only be an issue during training (and not inference). I'll see if I can reproduce the error.

**@HelloWorldLTY:** Hi, thanks for your catch up. Happy to offer more help if you need.

**@martinkim0:** Closing in favor of #2366

## #2343: Format timestamps for compatibility with new `fsspec`
**PR:** #2343 | **Last activity:** 2023-12-04T21:18:09Z
**State:** merged | **Author:** @martinkim0 | **Comments:** 0 | **Created:** 2023-12-04 | **URL:** https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2343

Logging directory formatting in `SaveCheckpoint` and `ModelTuner` was causing [errors](https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/actions/runs/7076214573/job/19259213548) since we use `-` and `:` for dates. Replacing these with `_` fixes the issue.

Looks like this was caused by `fsspec==2023.12.0` since [pinning](https://github.com/scverse/scvi-tools/pull/2344) to `2023.10.0` also fixes the issue. Not sure exactly what's in the new release that's causing this, but probably best to change the formatting. This is backwards compatible as auto-formatting of logging directories in `SaveCheckpoint` and `ModelTuner` were set to be released in 1.1.
